The Indians fired as they approached and mortally wounded one of his men, Mr. William B. Mekemson, a brave volunteer from St. Clair county, (whose father’s family afterwards settled in this, Henderson county, all of whom, except one brother, Andrew, a highly respected Christian gentleman, have, long since, gone to meet their kinsman in another world.) Mr. M. being unable to ride, a rude litter was made and men detailed to carry him back to camp, at Kellogg’s Grove. The company had not proceeded far before they were attacked by about seventy-five Indians, and two men, Scott and McDaniel, killed, and a Mr. Cornelius wounded. The company was soon formed into line by the aid of Gen. Whiteside, who was then acting merely as a private, and using the precaution of Indians, each man got behind a tree, and the battle waxed furiously for sometime without any serious results, until the Indian commander was seen to fall, from the well directed aim of Gen. Whiteside’s rifle. Having now no leader the Indians ingloriously fled, but for some reason were not pursued. Our reporter, however, said that most of the company refused, for the reason that the second term of their enlistment had expired, and they were anxious to be mustered out of service, although the officers were eager to pursue.

The company then commenced their march to camp, and on approaching the litter on which Mekemson lay, found that the Indians had cut off his head and rolled it down the hill. Soon after, Major Riley, with a small force of regulars, came up, and after consultation with Capt. Snyder, it was deemed best not to follow the retreating Indians, as their route probably led to the main army of Black Hawk.

===Chinese===
亨利上校团里的 A·W·斯奈德上尉连奉命警戒加利纳与福克斯河、罗克河之间的地带，6 月 17 日夜间在熊橡林地（Burr Oak Grove）附近扎营时突遭袭击。哨兵执勤时遭到印第安人枪击；对方认为不宜恋战，随即遁走。次日清晨，天刚亮得足以循踪追迹，斯奈德上尉便率连出发，可赶到敌方营地时，发现他们已闻风而逃。他加倍提速，循着踪迹穷追，终于赶上了他们。印第安人眼见无路可逃，便躲进一道深沟据守，但很快被包围，斯奈德上尉下令冲锋。印第安人趁我军逼近时开火，击中他部下一人要害——威廉·B·梅克姆森先生（Mr. William B. Mekemson），圣克莱尔县（St. Clair county）来的一名勇敢志愿兵（他父亲一家后来定居在本县，即亨德森县；除一个兄弟安德鲁——一位深受敬重的基督徒绅士——外，全家人多年前都已陆续去另一个世界与亲人团聚了）。梅克姆森先生无法骑马，众人便做了副简陋的担架，派专人把他抬回凯洛格林地的营地去。连队没走出多远，就遭到约七十五名印第安人攻击，两名士兵——斯科特（Scott）与麦克丹尼尔（McDaniel）——阵亡，一位科尼利厄斯先生（Mr. Cornelius）负伤。靠了怀特塞兹将军之力，连队很快整好了队形——将军当时自愿只当一名列兵；大家学着印第安人的谨慎，人人依托一树，战斗一度打得十分激烈，却无重大伤亡，直到印第安人的指挥官被怀特塞兹将军一记精准的枪法射倒。群龙无首的印第安人狼狈溃逃，但不知为何未遭追击。据本报随军记者说，连队大多数人都不肯追，理由是他们第二个服役期已满，急于退伍，尽管军官们求战心切。

连队随即启程回营；走近梅克姆森躺卧的担架时，才发现印第安人已割下他的头颅，滚下了山坡。不久，赖利少校（Major Riley）率一小队正规军赶到，与斯奈德上尉商议后，众人认为最好不必追赶溃逃的印第安人，因为他们走的路线很可能通向黑鹰的主力。

##### Apple River Fort / 苹果河堡

===Original===
On the 23rd of June scouts came into Galena, and reported at headquarters that a large body of Indians had been seen about thirty miles distant, but not being on the march, they were not able to conjecture to what point they were going. Col. Strode immediately made all necessary preparations to receive them, should Galena be the point of attack, and dispatched an express early next morning for Dixon’s Ferry. On their arrival at Apple River Fort they halted for a short time, and then proceeded on their journey, and while yet in sight, at the crack of a gun the foremost man was seen to fall from his horse and two or three Indians rushed upon him with hatchets raised ready to strike, while his comrades galloped up, and with guns pointed towards the Indians kept them at bay until the wounded man reached the Fort. But had the Indians known these guns were *not loaded*, (as afterwards reported) they could have dispatched all three of them with their tomahawks.

In a very short time after hearing the crack of the gun a large body of Indians surrounded the fort, yelling and shooting, when the inmates, under command of Capt. Stone, prepared for defence, every port hole being manned by sharpshooters. One man, Mr. George Herclurode, was shot through a port hole and instantly killed, and Mr. James Nutting wounded in the same way, but not seriously; which was the only loss sustained during the engagement of more than one hour’s duration. A number of Indians were wounded and carried off the field. Capt. Stone had only twenty-five men, with a large number of women and children in the fort, but had providentially received a quantity of lead and provisions from Galena only an hour before the attack, and as he was short of bullets, the ladies of the fort busied themselves in melting lead and running balls as long as the battle lasted. Black Hawk, finding the fort impregnable from assault without firing it﻿—an act that he well knew would, in a very short time, have brought a large body of troops on his path﻿—concluded that it would be better to return and carry with them all the flour they could, killed a number of cattle and took choice pieces of beef, and all the homes that were in the stable. One of the expressmen, not deeming the fort a place of safety, hurried back to Galena, but getting lost on the way did not get in until early next morning. On hearing the news, Col. Strode took one hundred picked men, well mounted, and went to the relief of the fort, and was much gratified to find that its noble defenders had put to flight about one hundred and fifty Indians who had been under the command of Black Hawk himself.

===Chinese===
6 月 23 日，侦察兵进入加利纳，向总部报告说，在约三十英里外发现大队印第安人，但他们并未开拔，因此无从揣测其去向。斯特罗德上校当即作好一切必要准备，以防加利纳成为攻击目标，并于次日一早派出急差前往迪克森渡口。信使们抵达苹果河堡（Apple River Fort）后稍事停留，随即继续赶路；他们还在视野之内时，一声枪响，最前面那人也随之坠马，两三个印第安人高举战斧向他扑去，眼看就要劈下；他的同伴们疾驰赶来，用枪指着印第安人，把对方逼住不敢上前，直到伤者进了堡。但据事后所悉，这些枪里*并未装弹*——印第安人若知道这一点，用战斧就能把三个人统统结果了。

听到枪声后不多时，大队印第安人便包围了堡垒，呐喊着、射击着；堡内的人在斯通上尉（Capt. Stone）指挥下奋起防守，每个射击孔后都布了神枪手。一名叫乔治·赫克拉罗德先生的人被子弹穿过射击孔击中，当场阵亡；詹姆斯·纳丁先生（Mr. James Nutting）也以同样方式负伤，但伤势不重——这是这场持续一个多小时的战斗中仅有的损失。印第安人方面有数人负伤，被抬离了战场。斯通上尉手下只有二十五人，堡中还有大批妇女儿童，所幸在袭击前一小时，上天眷顾，他刚从加利纳领到一批铅和口粮；他正缺弹丸，堡中的女士们便在整个战斗期间忙着熔铅铸弹。黑鹰见这堡垒强攻不下，若不纵火焚堡便无法攻克——他深知此举会在极短时间内引来大队官兵截其去路——便认定不如回去，于是尽其所能带走所有面粉，宰杀了若干头牛，取走上好的牛肉，还牵走了马厩里所有的马。一名信使觉得堡里不是安全之地，急忙折回加利纳，途中迷了路，直到次日清晨才回到镇上。斯特罗德上校闻讯，率一百名精选士兵，人强马壮，赶去救援堡垒；得知堡中英勇的守军已击退约一百五十名印第安人——他们正是由黑鹰亲自统率的——上校深感欣慰。

##### Kellogg’s Grove Fort / 凯洛格林地堡

===Original===
After leaving Apple River Fort, being well supplied with provisions, the Indians moved leisurely toward the fort at Kellogg’s Grove, with the intention of taking it, as scouts had come in and reported that it was not very strongly garrisoned on the day previous to their arrival on the 23rd of June. At this time the Illinois troops were rendezvoused at a place known as Fort Wilbourn on the Illinois river, at or near where now stands the city of LaSalle. What was then called the new levy, after Stillman’s defeat, were assembled there, numbering about three thousand men, being formed into military organizations consisting of three brigades. The first brigade was commanded by Gen. Alexander Posey. The second by Gen. M. K. Alexander, and the third by Gen. James D. Henry. Major John Dement, of Vandalia, was elected to the command of a spy battalion composed of three companies. Gen. Atkinson, of the United States Regulars, commanding, while these organizations were progressing.

The Indians had made a raid on Bureau creek, situated between the Illinois and Rock rivers. John Dement had been chosen major by the members of three companies of Gen. Posey’s brigade, which was a spy battalion. The Major’s battalion being ready for duty when the news reached the fort of the attack upon the settles on Bureau creek, it was ordered to march at once to the scene of danger for protection of the settlers, and to discover and watch the movements of the Indians, if possible. The Major was ordered to scour the country through to Rock river, and then to report to Col. Zackary Taylor, who commanded a small force of United States troops at a small fortification at Dixon’s Ferry on Rock river.

On the 22nd of June, 1832, Major Dement reached Colonel Taylor’s command, having performed the duties to which he was assigned by General Atkinson. On his arrival Colonel Taylor informed the Major that he had arrived at an opportune time, as he wished him to take his command, swim their horses across the river, and promptly occupy the country between his position and the Lead Mines at Galena, a distance of about sixty miles, with headquarters at Kellogg’s Grove, thirty-seven miles in the direction of Galena and Apple River Fort. There had been stationed at the grove two companies of Regulars, commanded by Major Riley, and three companies of Volunteers that had abandoned this position the day before the arrival of Major Dement, and left the country without protection and entirely unguarded. These troops had been engaged in two or three skirmishes with the Indians, and according to the reports of the soldiers, had been worsted in each. Major Dement’s command numbered one hundred and forty men, all told, not one of whom had ever seen any military experience, but they were men to be relied upon. They were citizen soldiers, brave and intelligent, equal to any emergency, and had no superiors in the service. This being an odd battalion, Major Dement was entitled to the staff of a Colonel. His staff was composed of Zadoc Casey, Paymaster; —﻿—﻿— Anderson, Colonel Hicks, and others. The Captains of the companies, and the staff officers, were leading citizens, who had, at short warning, left their several avocations to engage in defending the country against the attacks of the Indians.

===Chinese===
离开苹果河堡后，印第安人粮草充足，从容不迫地朝凯洛格林地的堡寨进发，打算把它拿下，因为已有侦察兵回来报告，说那里守备不甚雄厚——这是他们 6 月 23 日抵达前一天的情形。此时，伊利诺伊部队正集结在伊利诺伊河上一个叫威尔伯恩堡（Fort Wilbourn）的地方，即今日拉萨尔市（LaSalle）所在地或其附近。斯蒂尔曼之败后所谓的新征部队就集合在那里，约三千人，编为三个旅：第一旅由亚历山大·波西将军（Gen. Alexander Posey）指挥，第二旅由 M·K·亚历山大将军（Gen. M. K. Alexander）指挥，第三旅由詹姆斯·D·亨利将军（Gen. James D. Henry）指挥。万达利亚的约翰·德门特少校（Major John Dement）当选为一个侦察营的营长，该营由三个连组成。合众国正规军的阿特金森将军在这些部队编组期间总揽指挥。

印第安人曾袭掠比尤罗溪（Bureau creek）——该溪位于伊利诺伊河与罗克河之间。波西将军旅里三个连的官兵推选约翰·德门特为少校，他们那个旅编成一个侦察营。比尤罗溪畔移民点遭袭的消息传到堡中时，德门特少校的营正整装待命，奉令即刻开赴险地，保护移民，并尽可能侦察、监视印第安人的动向。少校奉命扫荡乡野，直抵罗克河，然后向扎卡里·泰勒上校（Col. Zackary Taylor）报到——泰勒在罗克河畔迪克森渡口的一座小型防御工事里，统率着一小支合众国部队。

1832 年 6 月 22 日，德门特少校完成了阿特金森将军分派的任务，抵达泰勒上校的驻地。泰勒上校告诉少校，他来得正是时候：上校希望他率领所部，泅马渡河，迅速进占其驻地与加利纳铅矿之间约六十英里的地带，司令部设于凯洛格林地——朝加利纳和苹果河堡方向三十七英里处。原先驻扎在林地的是赖利少校指挥的两个正规军连，另有三个志愿兵连，却在德门特少校抵达前一天弃守而去，任这一带无人护卫、全无防备。这些部队先前与印第安人交过两三次锋，据士兵们讲，每战皆北。德门特少校所部共一百四十人，没有一个有过行伍经历，但都是靠得住的人。他们是平民士兵，勇敢而明达，临变不惊，在军中无可匹敌。由于这是一个独立营，德门特少校得享上校的幕僚编制。他的幕僚包括薪饷官扎多克·凯西（Zadoc Casey）、一位名字失载的安德森（Anderson）、希克斯上校（Colonel Hicks）等人。各连连长和幕僚军官都是地方上的头面人物，一得通知，便放下各自营生，投身抵御印第安人来犯的战事。

##### Major Dement’s Battle with the Indians / 德门特少校与印第安人之战

===Original===
On the evening of the second day, after crossing Rock river, the Major’s command marched to the stockade at Kellogg’s Grove and encamped. In the morning, learning that Indian traces had been seen four or five miles from the grove, twenty-five volunteers were called for to go out and reconnoitre. This number was quickly filled, nearly everyone volunteering being an officer, and, as it afterward turned out, they were unfortunately accepted. These volunteers had not yet gotten out of sight of their camp, before three Indians were seen on their ponies between the fort and a small grove on the prairie, riding backward and forward. The reconnoitering party started after them in one, two and three order, according to the speed of their horses, while the Indians made straight for the small grove. Major Dement, who was watching the movements of the volunteers from his camp, and seeing the movements of the Indians, at once suspected a trap, mounted with a portion of his men, and went to their aid. His men that had first started were a mile out upon the prairie in pursuit of those few Indians. Being well mounted, the Major and his relief party soon overtook the hindermost of the little band, but several were too far in advance in their mad pursuit of the fleeing Indians for him to reach them in time. The fleeing Indians were making for a grove some three miles away, hotly pursued by the Major’s men. In this grove, as the commander feared, a large number of the Indians were concealed. When within four or five hundred yards of this grove he halted and dismounted his men and formed them in line. Some six or seven of his men were still in advance following the Indians toward this grove. On nearing the grove, his men who were in advance, were received with a galling fire, which killed two and wounded a third. With hideous yells the Indians emerged from the grove and rapidly approached. They were all mounted, stripped to their waists and painted for battle. As they reached the bodies of the dead soldiers, a large number surrounded them, clubbing and stabbing their lifeless remains. A volley from the rifles of the whites killed two or three at this point, but by the time the last of the little band had reached the ridge upon which their comrades were drawn up in line, the Indians were close upon them and on both flanks. At this point three men who had been out of their camp hunting for their homes, came in sight and were massacred in sight of their friends. The main portion of the battalion had been ordered to hold themselves in readiness for any emergency, but hearing the yelling, instead of obeying the order, mounted in hot haste and started to the rescue of their companions. On discovering the force of the Indians, they retreated to the grove, and almost neck and neck with the Indians, sprang over their horses and occupied the Block House.

On the least exposed side of the Fort was a work bench; over this the Major threw the bridle rein of his horse, and most of the horses huddled around this as if conscious of their danger. The Indians swarmed around the Block House under cover; an ominous stillness pervaded the air, which was soon broken by the crack of the rifles of the white men. The best marksmen with the best guns were stationed at the port holes, and a lively fire was kept up by the little garrison. The Indians finding that they were making no impression, turned their attention to shooting the horses, twenty-five of which they succeeded in killing. After sharp firing for two hours they retreated, leaving nine of their men dead on the field. This was the first engagement in this war, in which the whites had held their position until reinforcements arrived, without retreating. If the main force had remained in the grove at this Block House after the volunteers went out, without making any demonstration when the Indians came charging up and still in the open prairie, they could have been easily repulsed. This was the Major’s plan of action, but the men became excited by the firing, and having no commissioned officers to guide them, started without order to assist their exposed comrades in the open prairie, when they were flying for their lives to the block house.

That evening Gen. Posey came up with his brigade, and although the Indians were encamped a short distance away, he made no effort to attack them but contented himself with reporting the situation to Col. Z. Taylor at Dixon’s Ferry. Gen. Whiteside had said to Major Dement before crossing Rock river, that he was going into the Indian rendezvous, where he could have an Indian for breakfast every morning, and he found it literally true.

===Chinese===
渡过罗克河后的第二天傍晚，少校所部行军至凯洛格林地的栅堡扎营。清晨，得知在离林地四五英里处有人见过印第安人的踪迹，遂征召二十五名志愿者外出侦察。人数很快招满，报名者几乎个个是军官——事后证明，收下他们实为不幸。这些志愿者尚未走出营地视野，就看见三个印第安人骑着矮种马，在堡垒与草原上一小片林地之间来回驰骋。侦察队按照各自马速，一前两后地追了上去，印第安人则直奔那片小林地。德门特少校正在营中注视志愿者的动向，一见印第安人的行动，立刻疑心是个圈套，便带一部分人上马，赶去接应。他先派出的那批人已追着那几个印第安人冲出一英里，到了草原深处。少校和他的接应队马匹精良，很快追上那支小队的殿后人员，但另有几人疯狂追赶逃敌，冲得太前，他已来不及赶上。逃奔的印第安人直奔约三英里外的一片树林，少校的部下紧追不舍。正如这位指挥官所担心的，林中埋伏着大批印第安人。距树林四五百码时，他勒住队伍，令部下下马列阵。他手下还有六七人冲在前面，跟着印第安人朝树林去。这批前出的人临近树林时，遭到一阵猛烈的火力迎击，两人阵亡，一人负伤。印第安人发出可怖的呐喊冲出树林，迅速逼近。他们都骑着马，赤裸到腰部，涂着出战的花纹。他们赶到阵亡士兵的遗体旁，一大群人围拢上去，用枪托砸、用刀戳那无生命的躯体。白人步枪一轮齐射，又击毙两三人；但当这支小小队伍的最后几人登上同伴列阵的山脊时，印第安人已紧逼其后，并包抄了两翼。就在此时，三个离营寻家的人进入视野，当着朋友们的面惨遭屠戮。营部主力本奉命整装待变，但一听到呐喊声，便不再遵令，纷纷急急上马，赶去救援同伴。及见印第安人势大，他们退回林地这边，与印第安人几乎是马鬃相接，翻身跃下马背，抢占了木堡。

堡垒受敌最少的一侧有一张工作台；少校把缰绳搭在台上，大多数马都挤聚在台边，仿佛意识到自己的危险。印第安人借着掩体蜂拥到木堡四周；空气中弥漫着不祥的寂静，很快便被白人的步枪声划破。枪法最好、枪械最精的人守在射击孔后，这支小小的守军打得火力十足。印第安人见打不出战果，便转而射击马匹，成功地打死了二十五匹。激战两小时后，他们撤退了，丢下九具尸体在阵地上。这是这场战争中，白人第一次坚守阵地直到援军到来而未后退的一仗。倘若主力在志愿者出营后留在木堡旁的树林里，在印第安人冲上来、尚在开阔草原上时不动声色，本可轻而易举将来敌击退。这正是少校的作战方略，但士兵们被枪声激得兴奋起来，又没有军官节制，便擅自出发，去接应那些暴露在开阔草原上、正没命奔向木堡的同伴。

当天傍晚，波西将军率旅赶到；印第安人虽就扎营在近处，他却未作进攻的打算，只满足于把局势报告给迪克森渡口的 Z·泰勒上校。渡罗克河之前，怀特塞兹将军曾对德门特少校说，他这就要开进印第安人的老巢，每天早晨都能拿一个印第安人当早餐——后来他发现这话竟一点儿不假。

===Original===
It seems strange that Major Dement should have been ordered by Col. Taylor into the enemy’s country, across Rock river, with so small a force of volunteers, while a large force of Regulars and Volunteers, commanded by regular United States officers, remained securely entrenched in the rear. It was Major Dement’s opinion that there were more fighting men of Black Hawk’s band of warriors in the engagement at Kellogg’s Grove than ever afterwards made a stand during the war. It was easy for Gen. Posey to have moved up and attacked the Indians on his arrival at the Grove, and then have dealt them a fatal blow by forcing them to battle then, but he refused to do so, and the war was not terminated until the fight at Bad Axe some two months later, in which the Illinois troops did not engage. During this engagement at the Block House, four whites and eleven Indians were killed. The whites lost a large part of their horses﻿—the Indians shooting them from the timber, while the poor animals were huddled about the Block House.

Although in command, Black Hawk remained in the Grove doing the engagement, looking on to see that his principal aid, whose voice was like a trumpet call, carried out his orders.

While reciting the incidents of this battle to the author, when writing his autobiography, Black Hawk spoke in high praise of Major Dement as a commander, who had shown not only good military skill in coming to the rescue of his party, but in withdrawing his little party to the Fort.

After Dement’s engagement General Posey’s brigade started for Fort Hamilton and remained there a short time. News of Dement’s engagement and march of Posey’s brigade having been received at Dixon’s Ferry, where the two other brigades were stationed, Gen. Alexander, with the 2nd brigade was ordered to cross Rock river and march to Plum river to intercept the Indians, as it was deemed probable that they would make for that point to cross the Mississippi. Gen. Atkinson, with regulars, and Gen. Fry with his brigade, remained at Dixon waiting for news of the route taken by the Indians. Next day Capt. Walker and three Pottowottamie Indians came into Dixon and reported seventy-five Pottowottamies ready to join the army now encamped at Sycamore creek, and they were afraid that Black Hawk and his army was not far off. For their protection, and to await the coming of the balance of the second brigade, Col. Fry, of Henry’s brigade, was sent forward immediately. The next morning Gen. Henry’s brigade moved forward with Gen. Atkinson at the head, intending to march up Rock river, to the Four Lakes, and camped at Stillwell’s battleground the first night and joined Col. Fry and his Pottowottamie Indians on the 29th, and continued their march. On the 30th, when going into camp, they saw signs of Sac Indians, but the sentinels were undisturbed during the night. The next day they saw one Indian, but he was on the other side of Plum river. On the 2nd of July, Major Ewing being in front, spied a fresh trail, and soon after came upon the fresh trail of Black Hawk’s entire force, at a point near Keeshkanawy Lake. Scouts from the battalion came up to Black Hawk’s encampment, from which they had apparently taken their departure a few days before. Here they found five white men’s scalps which had been left hung up to dry. This battalion continued to march around the lake in detachments, one of which found where there had been another encampment, but on returning to camp and comparing notes they began to despair of finding the main body of Black Hawk’s army in that region. On the 5th of July, Gen. Atkinson with his army took a rest. During the day some scouts brought in an old Indian nearly blind and half famished with hunger, whom the Indians had left in their flight. After eating, Gen. Atkinson questioned him closely as to the whereabouts of Black Hawk and his army, but was satisfied from his replies and helpless condition, that he did not know, but on taking up his line of march the near morning, Gen. Atkinson did not leave him as the Indians had done, alone and without any means of subsistence, but left him an abundance of food, and as we afterwards learned, the old man recruited and afterwards got back to his tribe.

===Chinese===
德门特少校竟被泰勒上校派往敌境、渡过罗克河，只带着这么一小队志愿兵；而后方却驻扎着大批由合众国正规军官统领的正规军和志愿兵，安然固守于工事之内——这似乎不可思议。依德门特少校之见，黑鹰麾下武士中的善战之辈，参加凯洛格林地这一仗的，比此后整个战争中任何一次列阵迎敌的都多。波西将军本可以轻易地推进到林地便向印第安人进攻，逼其接战，给他们致命一击，但他拒绝这样做；于是战争又拖了约两个月，直到巴德阿克斯之战才告终结——那一仗伊利诺伊部队并未参战。木堡这一仗，白人死四人，印第安人死十一人。白人损失了大部分马匹——印第安人从树林里射击，而那些可怜的牲口正挤作一团团守在木堡四周。

黑鹰虽握有指挥之权，交战期间却留在树林里，从旁督视，以确保他的主要副手——那人的嗓音如号角一般——切实执行他的命令。

黑鹰向本书作者口述自传、讲到这次战斗的种种情节时，对德门特少校的指挥推崇备至——他率部救援出战友，又把这支小小的队伍撤回堡中，两着都显示出良好的军事才干。

德门特之役后，波西将军的旅开赴汉密尔顿堡，在那里驻了短短一段时日。德门特接战与波西旅开拔的消息传到另外两个旅的驻地迪克森渡口后，亚历山大将军奉命率第二旅渡过罗克河，向普拉姆河（Plum river）进军，以截击印第安人，因为估计他们很可能奔那一带去渡密西西比河。阿特金森将军率正规军、弗赖将军（Gen. Fry）率其旅留在迪克森，等候印第安人去向的消息。次日，沃克上尉（Capt. Walker）和三名波塔瓦托米族印第安人来到迪克森，报告说有七十五名波塔瓦托米人愿意加入此刻扎营于西卡莫尔溪的部队，又说他们担心黑鹰及其大军就在不远处。为保护这些波塔瓦托米人，并等候第二旅其余部队赶到，亨利旅的弗赖上校立即前出。次晨，亨利将军的旅以阿特金森将军为前导开拔，打算沿罗克河上行，进军四湖（Four Lakes）；第一夜在斯蒂尔韦尔战场（Stillwell's battleground）宿营，29 日与弗赖上校及其波塔瓦托米印第安人会合，继续进军。30 日扎营时，他们看见了索克族印第安人的踪迹，但哨兵彻夜未受惊扰。第二天他们望见一个印第安人，但他在普拉姆河对岸。7 月 2 日，尤因少校（Major Ewing）在前开路，发现一条新鲜踪迹，不久又撞见黑鹰全部人马留下的新鲜踪迹，地点在基什卡纳维湖（Keeshkanawy Lake）附近。营里的侦察兵赶到了黑鹰的宿营地，对方显然几天前才离开。他们在那里发现五张白人的头皮，被挂起来晾着。这个营分批绕湖行军，其中一支发现了另一处旧营地的所在；但回到营中汇总情况后，大家开始对在这一带找到黑鹰主力不抱希望了。7 月 5 日，阿特金森将军率全军休整。白天，几名侦察兵带回一个几乎全盲、饿得半死的印第安老人，是印第安人溃逃时丢下的。老人吃过东西后，阿特金森将军细细盘问黑鹰及其队伍的下落，但从他的回答和无助的境况断定他并不知情。不过次日清晨拔营时，阿特金森将军没有像印第安人那样把他丢下，任他孤身一人、无以为生，而是给他留下了充裕的食物。据我们后来所知，老人恢复了元气，后来回到了他的部族。

===Original===
On the evening of the 9th the army encamped at White Water, and the next morning Indians were seen on the other side of this stream which was not fordable, one of whom shot and wounded a regular. After breaking camp, Gen. Atkinson ordered a move up the river, and that night camped with his entire force﻿—all having met at the same point. Gen. Dodge’s corps had taken a Winnebago prisoner and brought him into camp for the purpose of finding out if he knew where Black Hawk’s forces were. He said they were encamped on an island near Burnt Village. Col. William S. Hamilton, a brave and honored son of Alexander Hamilton, in command of a company of Menomonees, who had joined the main army the day before, with Captain Early and his command, after scouring the island thoroughly, reported there were no Indians on the island.

Governor Reynolds, who had been on the march up Rock River with his volunteers and the main army, together with Colonel Smith, Major Sidney Breese and Colonel A. P. Field, left the army and came into Galena on the 12th, from whom we obtained our information of the movements of the army. They were firmly of the opinion that the Indians had taken to the swamps, and gotten entirely out of reach of the army, and that no farther danger need be apprehended. Colonel Field, who is an eloquent speaker, at the solicitation of Colonel Strode, although nearly worn out with hard marches, made an able and soul-stirring speech to our regiment, and a large number of the inhabitants of Galena.

At this time the army was nearly out of provisions, and Fort Winnebago, about seventy-five miles distant, the nearest point at which they could replenish. General Atkinson then ordered General Posey with his brigade, to Fort Hamilton, General Henry’s and Alexander’s brigade and General Dodge’s squadron to Fort Winnebago for provisions; and sent General Ewing and his regiment to Dixon with Colonel Dunn, who had been seriously wounded by one of his own sentinels, but who afterwards recovered. General Atkinson then built a fort near the camping ground, which was Fort Keeshkanong. General Alexander returned on the 15th with provisions to the fort, while Generals Dodge and Henry thought best to go with their commands to the head of Fox river, and while on the way stopped at a Winnebago village and had a talk with their head men, who assured them that Black Hawk was then at Cranberry Lake, a point higher up Rock river. After a consultation by the Generals, it was deemed best to send an express to General Atkinson at Fort Keeshkanong, to let him know of the information they had got, and their intention of moving on the enemy the next morning. Dr. Merryman, of Colonel Collins’ regiment, and Major Woodbridge, Adjutant of General Dodge’s corps, volunteered to go, and with Little Thunder, a Winnebago chief, as pilot, started out to perform this dangerous service, and after traveling a few miles, came on fresh Indian trails, which Little Thunder pronounced to have been made by Black Hawk’s party, and fearing that they would be intercepted, insisted on returning to camp. Night was then approaching, and having no guide to lead them forward, they reluctantly followed Little Thunder back to camp. Orders were then given for an early move next morning, and at daylight the bugle sounded, and the army moved onwards. The trail was followed for two days, leading for Four Lakes. On the second day, July 21st, scouts from General Dodge’s corps came in and reported Indians, and as a confirmation of the fact, Dr. A. K. Philleo exhibited a scalp that he had taken from the head of one that he had shot. Dr. Philleo was brave as the bravest, and whenever a scouting party started out to look for Indians (unless his services were required in camp), was always in the lead, and this being his first Indian, took his scalp, and sent it to the writer, with written instructions how to preserve it. To this end we handed over both to a deaf and dumb printer in the office, who boasted somewhat of his chemical knowledge, who spent considerable time for a number of days in following the Doctor’s instructions. After the killing of this Indian, some of the scouts discovered fresh signs of more Indians, and after pursuing it for some miles, Dr. Philleo and his friend Journey, equally as brave, being in the lead, espied two more Indians, when each picked his man and fired, and both fell; one of them, although badly wounded, fired as he fell, and wounded one of the scouts. The Doctor’s attention was now directed to his wounded companion, hence his second Indian was allowed to retain his scalp.

The scouts, finding that the trail was fresh, and the Indians were rapidly retreating, having strewed their trail with camp equipage, in order to facilitate their movements, sent an express back to camp, when the army hastily took up the line of march, with Dodge’s corps and Ewing’s Spy battalion in the front. By fast riding they soon came up with the Indians, whom they found already in line to receive them.

===Chinese===
9 日晚，全军在白水河（White Water）扎营；次日清晨，只见河对岸出现了印第安人——该河不可涉渡——其中一个开枪打伤一名正规军士兵。拔营后，阿特金森将军令全军沿河上溯，当夜全军同宿一处——各路人马都聚到了同一个地点。道奇的部队俘了一名温尼贝戈人，带进营来，想查明他是否知道黑鹰所部的下落。他说他们扎营在「焚村」（Burnt Village）附近的一座岛上。威廉·S·汉密尔顿上校（Col. William S. Hamilton）——亚历山大·汉密尔顿一位勇敢而受人尊敬的儿子——指挥着一连梅诺米尼族士兵，他们是前一天来与主力会合的；上校与厄尔利上尉（Captain Early）及其所部把那座岛彻底搜了一遍，报告说岛上并无印第安人。

雷诺兹州长一直带着他的志愿兵与主力一同沿罗克河上行，此番与史密斯上校（Colonel Smith）、西德尼·布里斯少校（Major Sidney Breese）和 A·P·菲尔德上校（Colonel A. P. Field）一道离开部队，于 12 日来到加利纳；军队动向的消息，我们便是从他们那里得来的。他们深信印第安人已遁入沼泽，远远摆脱了大军的追蹑，不必再担心有什么危险了。菲尔德上校是位雄辩的演说家，应斯特罗德上校之请，不顾连日艰苦行军、几近力竭，向我团以及加利纳众多居民发表了一篇有力而激动人心的演说。

此时全军口粮将尽，而约七十五英里外的温尼贝戈堡（Fort Winnebago）是最近的补给点。阿特金森将军遂命令波西将军率其旅前往汉密尔顿堡，亨利将军与亚历山大将军的两旅及道奇将军的骑兵队前往温尼贝戈堡筹粮；又派尤因将军率其团偕邓恩上校（Colonel Dunn）赴迪克森——邓恩上校被自己的一名哨兵重伤，后来方才痊愈。随后阿特金森将军在营地附近筑了一座堡，名唤基什卡农堡（Fort Keeshkanong）。亚历山大将军 15 日携粮返回堡中；道奇与亨利两位将军则认为最好率所部前往福克斯河河源，途中在一座温尼贝戈村落驻足，与村中头人们交谈；头人们言之凿凿，说黑鹰其时正在克兰伯里湖（Cranberry Lake）——罗克河再往上游的一处地方。几位将军计议之后，认为最好派一名急差去基什卡农堡，告知阿特金森将军他们所得的情报，以及明晨进击敌军的打算。柯林斯上校团里的梅里曼医生（Dr. Merryman）与道奇部队的副官伍德布里奇少校（Major Woodbridge）自告奋勇前往，带着一名温尼贝戈酋长「小雷」（Little Thunder）作向导，出发执行这项危险的差事；走出几英里，撞见了新鲜的大队印第安人踪迹，「小雷」断定是黑鹰一伙人所留；他生怕遭到截击，坚持要回营。夜幕将临，又没有别的向导引路，两人只得悻悻然跟着「小雷」折返。随即下达了次日一早开拔的命令，天一亮号角吹响，大军开动。沿着踪迹追了两日，方向直指四湖。第二天，即 7 月 21 日，道奇部队的侦察兵回来报告发现了印第安人；为证实此讯，A·K·菲利奥医生出示了一张头皮，是他从自己击毙的一个印第安人头上剥下的。菲利奥医生勇冠众人，每逢侦察队出发搜寻印第安人（除非营中需要他留用），他总走在最前头；这是他的第一个印第安人，他剥下头皮，寄给本文作者，还附了如何保存的书面说明。为此，我们把头皮和说明都交给了报馆里一名聋哑排字工——此君颇以自己的化学知识自许——他遵照医生的嘱咐，花了好些天的工夫摆弄。杀死这个印第安人之后，一些侦察兵又发现了更多印第安人的新鲜迹象；循迹追出几英里，菲利奥医生与同样勇敢的朋友乔尼（Journey）走在最前，望见又出现两个印第安人，两人各选一个目标开枪，双双命中；其中一个虽重伤倒地，仍在倒下时开枪打伤了我方一名侦察兵。医生此时一心照顾负伤的同伴，于是他的第二个印第安人才得以保住头皮。

侦察兵们见踪迹尚新，印第安人正迅速退走——为轻装疾行，沿途抛撒了营具——便派急差回报大营；大军随即匆匆开拔，道奇的部队和尤因的侦察营担任前锋。一路快马加鞭，很快追上了印第安人，只见他们已列好阵势等着接战。

##### At Wisconsin Heights / 威斯康星高地之战

===Original===
Orders were at once given to dismount (leaving enough to hold the horses) and charge upon the Indians. They had scarcely time to form into line when they were met by the yelling Indians and a heavy volley from their guns.

Dodge and Ewing ordered a charge, and as they moved forward, returned the fire at close quarters, with deadly effect. The Indians then commenced a flank movement, and by securing a position in the high grass where they could in a measure conceal themselves, fought bravely, until Dodge and Ewing gave orders to charge upon them at the point of the bayonet. In this engagement Col. Jones had his horse shot from under him, and one man killed﻿—but at the word “*charge*,” he went forward with his brave men, and all performed their duty nobly and fearlessly, and soon dislodged the Indians from their hiding place and forced them into a hasty retreat. It being then too late to pursue them, orders were given to camp on the battleground.

In this engagement Neapope had command, who was not only brave and fearless, but well skilled in strategy. Having become well acquainted with him after the war, he told the writer that he knew Gen. Dodge personally, and had met him on the field of battle, and considered him one of the bravest men he had ever met, although in this engagement all the officers showed great skill and bravery, and thus encouraged their men to acts of noble daring to a degree that he had never before witnessed in common﻿—not regular﻿—soldiers. He said in this engagement, the command had been entrusted to him of this small force﻿—about two hundred﻿—Indians, in order to give Black Hawk and the remainder of his party, time to cross the river. He reported his loss at twenty-eight killed.

The next morning a portion of the army was ordered forward to pursue the fleeing enemy, but on reaching the river, found that they had taken to the swamps, when it was deemed prudent to return to camp without attempting to follow them.

Here the army rested for one day, and made comfortable provisions to carry the wounded, after having consigned the remains of John Short, who had been killed the day before, to mother Earth, with the honors of war.

===Chinese===
立即下令下马（留足看马的人），向印第安人冲锋。我方还来不及列成横队，呐喊的印第安人和一阵密集的排枪便迎面而来。

道奇和尤因下令冲锋，部队边前进边在近距还击，杀伤惨重。印第安人随即开始翼侧机动，抢占高草丛中的一处阵地——那里多少可以藏身——奋勇作战，直到道奇和尤因下令端起刺刀冲锋。此役中琼斯上校（Col. Jones）的坐骑被打死，一名士兵阵亡——但一声「*冲锋*」令下，他带着麾下的勇士向前，人人恪尽职守，勇猛无畏，很快把印第安人赶出藏身之所，逼得他们仓皇后撤。其时天色已晚，不宜追击，遂下令在战场上扎营。

此役由尼亚波普（Neapope）指挥，他不仅勇猛无畏，而且深谙兵法。战后本文作者与他相熟，他告诉作者，他本人识得道奇将军，曾在战场上与他照面，认为他是自己平生所遇最勇敢的人之一；不过这一仗里，敌方军官个个显示出高超的胆略，激励部下作出豪勇之举，那种程度是他在普通士兵——不是正规军——身上从未见过的。他说，此役指挥权交到他手上，统率这支约二百人的小股印第安人队伍，为的是给黑鹰及其队伍的其余人马争取渡河的时间。他报出的损失是阵亡二十八人。

次晨，一部分军队奉命前出去追击溃逃的敌人，但赶到河边，发现敌人已遁入沼泽，便认为稳妥起见，不再尝试追蹑，返回了营地。

大军在此休整一日，安顿好运载伤员的稳妥办法；前一日阵亡的约翰·肖特（John Short）的遗体，则已按军礼下葬，托付给了大地母亲。

===Original===
In the meantime, Gen. Atkinson arrived with his regulars and the brigades of Generals Posey and Alexander; and on the 28th of July, took up the line of march with Gen. Atkinson at the head. Their route led through a mountainous country for several days, as the Indiana seemed to have selected the most difficult route they could find in order to gain time, and reach the river in advance, and then secure the best possible positions to defend themselves.

Having learned from an old Indian that had been left behind, that the enemy was only a short distance ahead, Gen. Atkinson, on breaking camp at an early hour in the morning, gave orders for the march towards the river, with Gen. Dodge’s squadron in front; Infantry next; Second brigade, under command of Gen. Alexander, next; Gen. Posey’s brigade next, and Gen. Henry’s in the rear.

After marching a few miles Gen. Dodge’s scouts discovered the rear guard of the enemy, when an express was sent immediately to Gen. Atkinson, who ordered troops to proceed at double quick. In the meantime Gen. Dodge’s command pushed forward and opened a heavy fire, from which many Indians were shot down while retreating toward the Mississippi, where their main body was stationed. Dodge’s squadron being in the lead, were first to open upon the main army of the Indians, whilst Gen. Henry’s brigade, that had been placed in the rear in the morning, came first to his aid. The battle waged furiously for more than two hours, and until the last visible Indian warrior was killed. The Indians had commenced crossing before the battle opened, and a number took to their canoes and made good their escape as the battle progressed. The number killed was estimated at something over one hundred, but the Indians afterward reported their loss at seventy-eight killed and forty-two wounded. Our loss was seventeen killed and about the same number wounded.

During the engagement several squaws were killed accidentally and a number wounded, including children, who were taken prisoners. Among the latter, Dr. Philleo reported a boy with one arm badly broken, who exhibited a greater degree of stoicism during the operation of amputation, than he had ever before witnessed. Being very hungry, they gave him a piece of bread to eat, which he ravenously masticated during the entire operation, apparently manifesting no pain whatever from the work of the surgeon.

Many of the Indians who got across the river in safety were afterwards killed by the Menomonees.

===Chinese===
其间，阿特金森将军率其正规军及波西、亚历山大两位将军的旅赶到；7 月 28 日，大军以阿特金森将军为前导开拔。一连数日，行军路线都在山地里穿行——印第安人似乎专挑了他们所能找到的最难走的路，以争取时间，抢先抵达河边，占住最有利的地势据以自守。

从一个掉队被弃的印第安老人那里得知敌人就在前方不远处后，阿特金森将军清早拔营时下令向河边进军：道奇将军的骑兵队为前锋，步兵继之，亚历山大将军指挥的第二旅随后，再是波西将军的旅，亨利将军的旅殿后。

行军数英里后，道奇的侦察兵发现了敌人的后卫，急差立刻驰报阿特金森将军，将军下令部队跑步前进。与此同时，道奇的部队奋力向前，开火猛烈，许多印第安人在向密西西比河退却途中中弹倒下——他们的主力正驻在河边。道奇的骑兵队一马当先，最先向印第安人的主力开火；清晨被置于队尾的亨利将军的旅，则最先赶来援应。这场血战激烈进行了两个多小时，直到最后一个看得见的印第安武士被击毙为止。交战开始前，印第安人已经开始渡河，战斗进行间，又有一些人乘上独木舟成功脱逃。击毙人数估计略过一百，但印第安人事后自报损失为阵亡七十八人、负伤四十二人。我方损失为阵亡十七人，负伤约与此相当。

交战期间，几名印第安妇人被误杀，另有一些负伤的，其中包括孩童，都做了俘虏。菲利奥医生提到俘虏中有一个男孩，一条手臂严重骨折，截肢手术过程中表现出的坚忍，超过了医生平生所见。孩子饿极了，人们给了他一块面包，整个手术期间他都在大口咀嚼着，对外科医生的刀下之功，似乎全无痛楚之色。

许多安全渡到河对岸的印第安人，后来被梅诺米尼人所杀。

##### Steamboat *Warrior*’s Fight / 汽船*「战士号」*之战

===Original===
On the 2nd of August, 1832, the steamboat, *Warrior*, was lying at Prairie du Chien, and word having been received at the fort that Black Hawk’s main army was then at, or near the river above, at a point designated for all to meet for the purpose of crossing the river, Lieut. Kingsbury took her in charge, and started up with one company, in order to intercept the Indians and prevent their crossing before the main army arrived, as he knew it was in close pursuit of them. The boat soon came in view of Indians on both sides of the river﻿—Black Hawk and several lodges having already crossed over﻿—when they were hailed by Lieut. Kingsbury. A white flag was hoisted by the Indians, and Black Hawk directed the Winnebago interpreter on board the *Warrior*, to say to his chief that he wanted him to send out his small boat so as he could go on board, as he desired to give himself up. The Winnebago, however, reported to the commander that they refused to bring their flag aboard. He then directed his interpreter to say that if they still refused he would open fire upon them. In reply, the interpreter said they still refused, when the Lieutenant directed his six-pounder to be fired among them, and also opened a musketry fire by his company. This was returned by the Indians, and the battle continued for some time. Several Indians were killed at the first fire, after which the remainder sought protection behind trees, stumps, etc. It was then getting late in the afternoon, and as the boat was nearly out of wood they dropped down to the fort to replenish, and started back again the next morning. On reaching an island some miles above their battleground of the day before, they commenced to rake it with their six-pounder, supposing the Indians had taken shelter there, and the army considering it a salute, Gen. Atkinson returned it. Soon after the boat landed and took on board Gen. Atkinson and the regulars and then returned to Prairie du Chien. The Illinois volunteers were ordered to Dixon, at which place they were discharged, while the troops of the lead mines were mustered out at Galena. After the boat started down the evening before, Black Hawk and a few of his people left for the lodge of a Winnebago friend, and gave himself up. Thus ended a bloody war which had been forced upon Black Hawk by Stillman’s troops violating a flag of truce, which was contrary to the rules of war of all civilized nations, and one that had always been respected by the Indians. And thus, by the treachery or ignorance of the Winnebago interpreter on board of the *Warrior*, it was bought to a close in the same ignoble way it commenced﻿—disregarding a flag of truce﻿—and by which Black Hawk lost more than half of his army. But in justice to Lieut. Kingsbury, who commanded the troops on the *Warrior*, and to his credit it must be said, that Black Hawk’s flag would have been respected if the Winnebago, who acted as his interpreter on the boat, had reported him correctly.

===Chinese===
1832 年 8 月 2 日，汽船*「战士号」*（*Warrior*）正停泊在普雷里迪欣。堡中得到消息说，黑鹰的主力此刻在上游河边、或在河边近处，正聚在众人约定渡河的地点；金斯伯里中尉（Lieut. Kingsbury）受命接管此船，带一个连溯流而上，以截击印第安人，赶在主力大军到达之前阻止他们渡河——他知道大军正在紧追敌人。船行不久，便望见两岸都有印第安人——黑鹰和几户帐篷的人已经渡到对岸——金斯伯里中尉向他们呼喊问话。印第安人升起一面白旗，黑鹰请*「战士号」*船上的温尼贝戈译员转告船上的指挥官：他想请对方放出小艇，好让他上船，因为他愿意投降。然而那温尼贝戈人向指挥官报告的，却是他们不肯把旗送上船来。中尉便让译员传话：再不从命，便要开火。译员回报说他们仍旧拒绝，中尉遂令六磅炮向人群轰击，连队也一齐开了排枪。印第安人还击，战斗持续了一阵。第一轮炮火便打死数名印第安人，余下的躲到树木、树桩等后面避弹。时已下午向晚，船上的柴又快烧尽，便顺流下驶回堡补给，次日清晨再驶回来。行到前日战场上方几英里的一座岛边，他们以为印第安人藏身岛上，便用六磅炮沿着岛纵列扫射；岸上大军以为是礼炮，阿特金森将军也鸣炮回礼。不久船靠岸，接阿特金森将军与正规军上船，返回普雷里迪欣。伊利诺伊志愿兵奉命开赴迪克森，在那里解散；铅矿的部队则在加利纳退伍。前一晚船下驶之后，黑鹰带着少数族人前往一位温尼贝戈朋友的棚屋，投降归案。这场血腥的战争就这样结束了——战争本是斯蒂尔曼的部队践踏休战白旗强加于黑鹰的，此举违背一切文明国家的战争规则，而白旗向来是印第安人所尊重的。而战争的收场，又因*「战士号」*船上那名温尼贝戈译员的奸诈或无知，竟以与开端同样卑劣的方式——无视休战白旗——告终；黑鹰也由此折损了大半人马。但为*「战士号」*上统兵的金斯伯里中尉计、为其令誉计，必须说明：倘若船上充任译员的那名温尼贝戈人如实转达，黑鹰的白旗本会得到尊重。

##### General Atkinson’s Report / 阿特金森将军的报告

===Original===
**Headquarters First Artillery Corps, Northwestern Army**, Prairie du Chiens, Aug. 25, 1832.

**Sir**:﻿—I have the honor to report to you that I crossed the Ouisconsin on the 27th and 28th ultimo, with a select body of troops, consisting of the regulars under Colonel Taylor, four hundred in number, part of Henry’s, Posey’s and Alexander’s brigades, amounting in all to 1,300 men, and immediately fell upon the trail of the enemy, and pursued it by a forced march, through a mountainous and difficult country, till the morning of the 2nd inst., when we came up with his main body on the left bank of the Mississippi, nearly opposite the mouth of the Ioway, which we attacked, defeated and dispensed, with a loss on his part of about a hundred and fifty men killed, thirty men, women and children taken prisoners﻿—the precise number could not be ascertained, as the greater potion was slain after being forced into the river. Our loss in killed and wounded, which is stated below, is very small in comparison with the enemy, which may be attributed to the enemy’s being forced from his position by a rapid charge the commencement, and throughout the engagement the remnant of the enemy, cut up and disheartened, crossed to the opposite side of the river, and had fled into the interior, with a view, it is supposed, of joining Keokuk and Wapello’s bands of Sacs and Foxes.

The horses of the volunteer troops being exhausted by long marches, and the regular troops without shoes, it was not thought advisable to continue the pursuit; indeed, a stop to the further effusion of blood seemed to be called for, till it might be ascertained if the enemy would surrender.

It is ascertained from our prisoners that the enemy lost in the battle of the Ouisconsin sixty-eight killed and a very large number wounded; his whole loss does not fall short of three hundred. After the battle on the Ouisconsin, those of the enemy’s women and children, and some who were dismounted, attempted to make their escape by descending that river, but judicious measures being taken by Captain Loomis and Lieutenant Street, Indian Agent, thirty-two women and children and four men have been captured, and some fifteen men killed by the detachment under Lieutenant Ritner.

The day after the battle on the river, I fell down with the regular troops to this place by water, and the wounded men will join us today. It is now my purpose to direct Keokuk to demand a surrender of the remaining principal men of the hostile party, which, from the large number of women and children we hold prisoners, I have every reason to believe will be complied with. Should it not, they should be pursued and subdued, a step Major-General Scott will take upon his arrival.

I cannot speak too highly of the brave conduct of the regular and volunteer forces engaged in the last battle, and the fatiguing march that preceded it, and as soon as the reports of officers of the brigades and corps are handed in, they shall be submitted with further remarks:

- 5 killed, 6 wounded, 6th inft.

- 2 wounded, 5th inft.

- 1 Captain, 5 privates, Dodge’s Bat., mounted.

- 1 Lieutenant, 6 privates, Henry’s Bat.

- 1 private wounded, Alexander’s.

- 1 private wounded Posey’s.

I have the great honor to be, with great respect,

Your obedient servant,

H. Atkinson,

Brevet Brig. Gen. U.S.A.

Maj. Gen. Macomb, Com. in Chief, Washington.

===Chinese===
**西北军第一炮兵军团司令部**，普雷里迪欣，1832 年 8 月 25 日。

**阁下**：——我谨向阁下报告：我于上月 27 日及 28 日，率一支精锐部队渡过威斯康星河（Ouisconsin），部队由泰勒上校麾下 400 名正规军及亨利、波西、亚历山大三旅之一部组成，总计 1,300 人；随后立即咬上敌人踪迹，强行军穿越山地险阻，直至本月 2 日晨，在密西西比河左岸、差不多正对艾奥瓦河（Ioway）河口之处追上敌军主力。我军当即攻击，将其击溃击散；敌方损失约 150 人阵亡，男女及孩童 30 人被俘——确数无从查证，因为大部分人是在被逼入河中之后被杀的。我方伤亡（详列于后）与敌方相比极为轻微，这可归因于开战之初的迅疾冲锋便将敌军逐出了阵地；整个交战过程中，残存的敌军溃乱丧胆，渡到河对岸，遁入内陆——据推测，意在投奔基奥卡克与瓦佩洛（Wapello）的索克族与福克斯族部落。

志愿兵部队的马匹经长途行军已疲惫不堪，正规军又无鞋可穿，故而认为不宜继续追击；况且，在查明敌人是否愿意投降之前，似也应停止再流鲜血。

据我方俘虏供称，敌军在威斯康星河一役中损失 68 人阵亡、伤者甚众；其全部损失不下 300 人。威斯康星河之战后，敌军的妇孺及一些失去坐骑的人试图顺流而下逃走，但卢米斯上尉（Captain Loomis）与印第安事务官斯特里特中尉（Lieutenant Street）处置得宜，32 名妇孺与 4 名男子被俘，另有约 15 名男子被里特纳中尉（Lieutenant Ritner）的分队击毙。

河上交战次日，我偕正规军由水路下驶至此地，伤员今日将与我们会合。我现拟指令基奥卡克，要求敌对一方余下的首要人物出面投降；鉴于我方手中俘虏妇孺甚众，我有充分理由相信此要求定获遵从。倘若不然，即应追剿降服——斯科特少将（Major-General Scott）到达后将采取这一步骤。

对于参加上一役的正规军与志愿兵部队的英勇表现，以及此前那次疲惫行军中的表现，我无论怎样称道都不为过；一俟各旅、各军团的军官报告呈报上来，即将附上进一步评语一并呈送：

- 第 6 步兵团：阵亡 5 人，负伤 6 人。
- 第 5 步兵团：负伤 2 人。
- 道奇乘骑营：上尉 1 员，士兵 5 员。
- 亨利营：中尉 1 员，士兵 6 员。
- 亚历山大旅：士兵 1 人负伤。
- 波西旅：士兵 1 人负伤。

我怀着极大的荣幸与敬意，

恭为阁下之忠仆，

H·阿特金森

合众国陆军荣誉准将（Brevet Brig. Gen. U.S.A.）

呈：华盛顿总司令马科姆少将（Maj. Gen. Macomb, Com. in Chief）

## Advertisement / 广告

===Original===
It is presumed that no apology will be required for presenting to the public the life of a Hero who has lately taken such high rank among the distinguished individuals of America. In the following pages he will be seen in the character of a Warrior, a Patriot and a State prisoner; in every situation he is still the chief of his Band, asserting their rights with dignity, firmness and courage. Several accounts of the late war having been published, in which he thinks justice is not done to himself or nation, he determined to make known to the world the injuries his people have received from the whites, the causes which brought on the war on the part of his nation, and a general history of it throughout the campaign. In his opinion this is the only method now left him to rescue his little Band, the remnant of those who fought bravely with him, from the effects of the statements that have already gone forth.

The facts which he states, respecting the Treaty of 1804, in virtue of the provisions of which the government claimed the country in dispute and enforced its arguments with the sword, are worthy of attention. It purported to cede to the United States all of the country, including the village and cornfields of Black Hawk and his band, on the east side of the Mississippi. Four individuals of the tribe, who were on a visit to St. Louis to obtain the liberation of one of their people from prison, were prevailed upon, says Black Hawk, to make this important treaty, without the knowledge or authority of the tribes, or nation.

In treating with the Indians for their country, it has always been customary to assemble the whole nation; because, as has been truly suggested by the Secretary of War, the nature of the authority of the chiefs of the tribe is such, that it is not often that they dare make a treaty of much consequence, and we might add, never, when involving so much magnitude as the one under consideration, without the presence of their young men. A rule so reasonable and just ought never to be violated, and the Indians might well question the right of the Government to dispossess them, when such violation was made the basis of its right.

The Editor has written this work according to the dictation of Black Hawk, through the United States Interpreter, at the Sac and Fox Agency of Rock Island. He does not, therefore, consider himself responsible for any of the facts, or views, contained in it, and leaves the Old Chief and his story with the public, whilst he neither asks, nor expects, any fame for his services as an amanuensis.

The Editor

===Chinese===
兹将一位新近跻身美国杰出人物之列的英雄的生平公之于众，想必无须任何致歉之辞。在以下篇章中，读者将看到他身为战士、爱国者与政治犯的形象；无论身处何种境地，他始终是自己部众的首领，以尊严、坚毅和勇气捍卫着族人的权利。此前已有数种关于晚近这场战争的记述刊行于世，但他认为其中对他本人及其民族都欠公允，于是决意让世人知晓他的族人自白人手中蒙受的种种侵害、他的民族之所以兴战的缘由，以及这场战事自始至终的全貌。在他看来，这是他如今仅剩的办法，好使他那支小小的部众——那些曾与他并肩英勇作战者所余的残部——免受业已流布于世的种种说法之害。

他所述关于 1804 年条约的各项事实值得留意：政府正是援引该条约的各项条款，声称对争议之地享有主权，并以刀剑推行其主张。该条约名义上将密西西比河以东的全部国土让与美国，其中包括黑鹰及其部众的村落与玉米田。据黑鹰说，该部族有四人当时正前往圣路易斯，为的是营救一名身陷囹圄的族人，却在诸部族乃至整个民族既不知情、亦未授权的情况下，被劝诱缔结了这项重要条约。

与印第安人交涉购买其土地，历来惯例是召集全族大会；因为，正如陆军部长所确切指出的，部族首领的权力性质决定了他们很少敢于缔结事关重大的条约；而且我们可以补上一句，若事关如此重大——一如本案所涉及的条约——而无族中青年在场，则断然不会有此举。如此合情合理的规则本绝不容违犯；而当这种违犯竟被奉为政府权利主张的依据时，印第安人大可质疑政府剥夺其土地的权利。

本书系编者依据黑鹰口述、经合众国译员转译，在罗克岛的索克-福克斯族事务代办处笔录而成。因此，编者自认对书中任何事实或观点概不负责，而将这位老首领及其故事交由公众评断；至于自己作为笔录者所尽的辛劳，则既不求名，亦不望誉。

编者

## Affidavit / 宣誓书

===Original===
> Be it remembered, that on this sixteenth day of November, Anno Domini eighteen hundred and thirty-three, J. B. Patterson, of said district, hath deposited in this office the title of a Book, the title of which is in the words following, to wit:
> “Life of Ma-ka-tai-me-she-kia-kiak, or Black Hawk, embracing the Traditions of his Nation﻿—Indian Wars in which he has been engaged﻿—Cause of joining the British in their late War with America, and its History﻿—Description of the Rock River Village﻿—Manners and Customs﻿—Encroachments by the Whites contrary to Treaty﻿—Removal from his village in 1831. With an account of the Cause and General History of the Late War, his Surrender and Confinement at Jefferson Barracks, and Travels through the United States. Dictated by himself.”
> J. B. Patterson, of Rock Island, Illinois, Editor and Proprietor.
> The right whereof he claims as author, in conformity with an act of Congress, entitled “An act to amend the several acts respecting copyrights.”
> **W. H. Brown**, 

 Clerk of the District of Illinois

> 《马-卡-泰-米-谢-基亚-基亚克，即黑鹰生平：内载其部族之传说——其亲历之印第安诸战——晚近英美战争中其投效英方之缘由与经过——罗克河村落之记述——其风俗习尚——白人违约之侵逼——1831 年被逐离本村之始末；并附晚近战争之起因与通史、其投降就缚及被押于杰斐逊兵营之情状、其遍游合众国之经历。皆由其本人口述。》
> J·B·帕特森，伊利诺伊州罗克岛人，编者兼所有人。

> **W·H·布朗**（W. H. Brown），

===Original===
> I do hereby certify, that Ma-ka-tai-me-she-kia-kiak, or Black Hawk, did call upon me, on his return to his people in August last, and expressed a great desire to have a History of his Life written and published, in order (as he said) “that the people of the United States, (among whom he had been traveling, and by whom he had been treated with great respect, friendship and hospitality,) might know the *cause* that had impelled him to acts as he had done, and the *principles* by which he was governed.”
> In accordance with his request, I acted as Interpreter; and was particularly cautious to understand distinctly the narrative of Black Hawk throughout﻿—and have examined the work carefully since its completion, and have no hesitation in pronouncing it strictly correct, in all its particulars.
> Given under my hand, at the Sac and Fox agency, the day and date above written.
> **Antoine Le Clair**, U.S. Interpreter for the Sacs and Foxes

===Chinese===
> 兹郑重证明：马-卡-泰-米-谢-基亚-基亚克，即黑鹰，于今年八月返回族人处时曾来见我，并表达了一项强烈的愿望——请人将其生平写成历史并予以刊行，以求（如他所言）「合众国的人民——他曾游历于他们中间，并蒙他们以极大的尊重、友谊与款待相待——得以知晓是何*缘由*驱使他做出昔日之举，又是凭何种*原则*行事」。
> 应其所请，我担任转译；自始至终格外用心，务必清楚理解黑鹰的叙述；书成之后，我又细加审校，可以毫无迟疑地断言：本书各项内容，均翔实无误。
> 谨此亲署于索克与福克斯族事务代办处，日期同上。
> **安托万·勒克莱尔**（Antoine Le Clair），索克与福克斯族合众国译员

## Appendix / 附录

### At Yellow Banks / 在黄岸

===Original===
Among the many hundreds of troops that came to Yellow Bank﻿—Oquawka﻿—on their way to the sea of war, Major S. S. Phelps always spoke in high terms of their good discipline and gentlemanly conduct, except in one instance﻿—that of a few persons in a company from McDonough county, who came over at a time when old chief Tama and his wife, who was noted for being the white man’s friend, came over to get provisions for his little band. On seeing an Indian some of these soldiers, who had been using their canteens rather frequently, were eager to slay him, and not only threatened him but Major P. also, for harboring him. The officers seemed to have no control of these men﻿—and just at a time when their threats were loudest of what they intended to do at the close of three minutes, Major P. and one of his clerks, Mr. Joseph Smart, were standing with their rifles cocked ready to make the first shot, a cry came from outside of the building, by one of the more peaceable soldiers, “Here comes another company, Capt. Peter Butler’s, from Monmouth,” when these would-be braves instantly retreated.
We are assured by one of Capt. B.’s company, Mr. James Ryason, that the foregoing is literally true, and that Major P. and Mr. Smart, afterwards, amid the threats of these same soldiers, escorted Tama and wife to the river bank to take their canoe to cross the river, and stood there with their guns, ready to protect the Indians until they got out of reach of gunshot﻿—Smart threatening all the time to put a ball though the first man that attempted to shoot.
In order to appease the wrath of these soldiers and prevent some of them being killed, Capt. B. advised Maj. P. not to give Tama any provisions; but on the way down, Mr. Ryason says, Smart (who talked their language equal to a native born) told them to meet them at a certain point after night and they would be supplied; and that for the purpose of assisting Mr. Smart in taking supplies to Tama, he got leave of absence from the Captain until next morning.
Messrs. James Ryason and Gabriel Shot, both honorable and highly respected Christian gentlemen, are the only survivors of that company now residing in this county.

===Chinese===
在取道黄岸——奥夸卡（Oquawka）——开赴「战争之海」的数以百计的部队当中，S·S·菲尔普斯少校（Major S. S. Phelps）对他们良好的军纪与绅士的行止总称许有加，唯有一例除外：麦克多诺县（McDonough county）某连队里的几个人，偏偏在老酋长塔马（Tama）和他的妻子——她素以亲善白人而闻名——过河来为他那一小队人领取口粮的时候来到这里。这些士兵中有几个人把水壶里的酒传得太勤，一见印第安人便起了杀心，不但威胁那印第安人，还威胁收容他的菲少校（Major P.），斥他包庇窝藏。军官们似乎全然管束不住这些人——正当他们叫嚣得最凶、扬言三分钟一到便要动手之际，菲少校和他的一名文书约瑟夫·斯马特先生（Mr. Joseph Smart）端着扳开枪机的步枪站在那里，准备抢先开火；忽听得楼外一名较为安分的士兵喊道：「又开进来一个连队——彼得·巴特勒上尉（Capt. Peter Butler）的，从蒙茅斯（Monmouth）来的」——这些自诩的勇士顿时缩了回去。

巴上尉（Capt. B.）连队里的一位詹姆斯·赖森先生（Mr. James Ryason）向我们保证，上述情节千真万确；他还说，菲少校和斯马特先生后来正是在这同一批士兵的威胁声中，护送塔马夫妇到河岸边乘独木舟渡河，并端着枪站在那里，随时准备保护这两位印第安人，直到他们走出枪弹射程——斯马特一路放话，谁敢开枪，他就先叫一颗子弹穿过谁的身子。

为了平息这些士兵的怒火、免得他们中有人丧命，巴上尉劝菲少校不要发给塔马任何口粮；但据赖森先生说，在顺河而下的路上，斯马特（他说起印第安语来同土生土长的人一样地道）暗地里让他们入夜后到某个地点会合，届时自会供给；而为了帮斯马特先生把口粮送去给塔马，他还向巴上尉告了假，直到次日清晨方回。

詹姆斯·赖森与加布里埃尔·肖特（Gabriel Shot）两位先生，都是正直而深受敬重的基督徒绅士，如今是那个连队中定居本县的仅存之人。

===Original===
Tama’s village, located on South Henderson, half a mile below the farm of Mr. John T. Cook, at Gladstone, was always noted as being the abode of friendly Indians. In the fall of 1829, some white men came in and made improvements on the land in the vicinity, and at the advice of Mr. Phelps, Tama crossed the river and made a new town at the mouth of Flint river on the Mississippi, and at the time of Black Hawk’s raid into Illinois, it was the rendezvous of many young men who had been persuaded by Tama not to join Black Hawk. But when the news reached them of the indignities offered to their good old chief, they secretly determined to go upon the war path, and soon after four young Foxes started to cross the river and avenge the insult. On going up Henderson creek they espied Mr. William Martin while in the act of mowing, at a point near Little York, whom they shot and killed, and for fear of detection, immediately took to the brush. It being late when they got through the woods, they made a fire and camped just at the edge of the prairie.
Some time after the shooting, friends of Mr. Martin discovered his lifeless body and after removing it to the home, started on the trail of his murderers, and followed it some distance through the underbrush, but wisely concluded, as it was growing late, to return and give the alarm. An express was sent to Capt. Butler during the night, who started out with his company early in the morning, and on emerging into the prairie discovered the camp fire of the Indians, and followed their trail to a slough in the Mississippi two miles below Keithsburgh. Here the Indians embarked in their canoes and were probably on the other side of the river by this time. A demand was immediately made upon Keokuk for the murderers, as they belonged to his band of Foxes, who surrendered two men to the commanding officer at Rock Island.
These Indians soon afterwards made their escape, and before the time fixed for their trial, Keokuk delivered four young men to Maj. Phelps, then sheriff of Warren county, to be tried for the offence. Maj. P. and his deputy, Mr. James Ryason, took them to Monmouth jail, where the following proceedings were had before the Circuit Court (for a copy of which we are indebted to George C. Rankin, Esq., now Circuit Clerk):

===Chinese===
塔马的村子坐落在南亨德森河（South Henderson）畔、格拉德斯通（Gladstone）约翰·T·库克先生（Mr. John T. Cook）农庄下游半英里处，向来以友善印第安人的居地著称。1829 年秋，一些白人进来在附近土地上垦拓经营；塔马听从菲尔普斯先生的劝告渡过河去，在密西西比河畔燧石河（Flint river）口另建了一座新镇。黑鹰进袭伊利诺伊之际，那里聚着许多经塔马劝说而没有去追随黑鹰的年轻人。但当他们听闻他们善良的老酋长所受的凌辱，便暗下决心踏上征伐之路；不久，四个福克斯族青年动身渡河，要去洗雪这一侮辱。他们沿亨德森河上行，在小约克（Little York）附近一处地方望见威廉·马丁先生（Mr. William Martin）正在割草，便开枪把他打死；因惧怕被人察觉，立即钻进了树丛。穿出树林时天色已晚，他们便生起火来，在草原边上就地宿营。

枪案发生过后一阵，马丁先生的朋友们发现了他毫无生气的遗体；将遗体抬回家中之后，便循着凶手的踪迹追去，在灌木丛中跟进了一段路，但天色渐晚，他们审慎地决定折回报警。当夜即有信使驰报巴特勒上尉；翌日清晨，他率连队出动，一进入草原就发现了印第安人的营火，便循迹追至基茨堡（Keithsburgh）下游两英里处密西西比河边的一片沼泽。印第安人在那里登上独木舟，此时多半已到河对岸去了。因凶手属于基奥卡克麾下的福克斯族部众，当局随即向基奥卡克索要凶手，他交出两人，由罗克岛的指挥官收押。

这两名印第安人不久便逃脱了。开审之期未到，基奥卡克又交出四名青年，送给当时任沃伦县（Warren county）治安官的菲尔普斯少校，听候此罪的审判。菲少校和他的副手詹姆斯·赖森先生把他们押解到蒙茅斯监狱，在巡回法院进行了如下的程序（其文书副本承现任巡回法院书记官乔治·C·兰金先生（George C. Rankin, Esq.）惠赐，谨此志谢）：

===Original===
> William Martin was shot and scalped by two Indians, near Little York, Warren county, August 9th, 1832. In their report at the October term of the Warren Circuit Court, the Grand jurors say:
> “Six or seven Indians of Keokuk’s band of Sac and Fox Indians who were not included in the war path under Black Hawk and other chiefs of the Sac and Fox nation, came over from the western bank of the Mississippi river to the inhabited parts of Warren county, in said State, and unlawfully and feloniously murdered the said William Martin in the most barbarous manner. That the names of the said Indians are unknown to the Grand Jury. That two of the said Indians have been heretofore given up by the chiefs of said Indians, that they were confined in the Fort at Rock Island for some time but have made their escape, and are now at large in their own country. That the Grand jury cannot now find an indictment because the names of the said Indiana are unknown to said jury. But they recommend that the Governor of the State be furnished with a copy of this presentment, and that he be desired to request of the President of the United States that the whole of the said Indians concerned in the said murder may be demanded of the said Sac and Fox nation that they may be indicted and punished for murder under the authority of the laws of this State.”
> In compliance with the demand of the President, the chiefs surrendered four Indians, namely, with their Interpretations;
> Sa-sa-pi-ma (he that troubleth).
> Ka-ke-mo (he that speaks with something in his mouth).
> I-o-nah (stay here).
> Wa-pa-sha-kon (the white string).
> Concerning which, the Grand jury at the June term 1833 say:
> “From an examination made by this Grand Jury they are now able to state that the four Indians lately surrendered by the chiefs at the request of the President of the United States, are not the real murderers of Martin. The chiefs represent that at the time the demand was made the real offenders had escaped from the territory and power of their nation. That the prisoners now in custody volunteered themselves to be surrendered in place of those who escaped, and that from custom amongst Indians, they supposed this would be a sufficient compliance with the requisition of the President. The Grand jury will not positively say that the chiefs have prevaricated, but they do say that the demand already made has been eluded.”
> By a writ of habeas corpus, the four Indians above named were brought before the judge, presiding, Hon. Richard M. Young, June 14th, 1833, and released.
> Indictment was returned against the real murderers, Shash-quo-washi, Muck-que-che-qua, Muck-qua-pal-ashah, and Was-a-wau-a-quot, who, “not having the fear of God before their eyes, but being moved and seduced by the instigations of the devil,” killed Wm. Martin. The indictment was drawn by Thomas Ford, States Attorney, and recites that William Martin was shot a little below the shoulder blade. Among the witnesses named were Keokuk and Stabbing Chief. The guilty parties were never arrested, and a *nolle prosequi* was entered at the October term at court, 1835.

===Chinese===
> 威廉·马丁于 1832 年 8 月 9 日在沃伦县小约克附近被两名印第安人枪杀并剥去头皮。在沃伦县巡回法院十月庭期的报告中，大陪审团陈称：

> 「有六七名印第安人，属基奥卡克所辖索克-福克斯族部众，并未随黑鹰及索克-福克斯族其他酋长踏上征伐之路；他们从密西西比河西岸渡河，进入本州沃伦县有人居住的地带，以最野蛮的方式非法而蓄意地杀害了上述威廉·马丁。上述印第安人的姓名，大陪审团无从知晓。上述印第安人中有两名此前已由其酋长交出，曾在罗克岛的堡垒中被关押一时，但已逃脱，如今逍遥于其本族境内。因上述印第安人姓名不为本陪审团所知，大陪审团现无从提起公诉。但大陪审团建议：应将本报告副本送达本州州长，并请其提请合众国总统，向上述索克-福克斯族索交参与上述谋杀的全部印第安人，使其得依本州法律之权限，以谋杀罪被起诉、惩办。」

> 遵照总统的要求，酋长们交出了四名印第安人，其名字连同各自的释义如下：

> 萨-萨-皮-马（Sa-sa-pi-ma，意为「制造纷扰者」）。

> 卡-克-莫（Ka-ke-mo，意为「口中含物而言者」）。

> 伊-奥-纳（I-o-nah，意为「留在此地」）。

> 瓦-帕-沙-孔（Wa-pa-sha-kon，意为「白绳」）。

> 关于此事，大陪审团在 1833 年六月庭期陈称：

> 「经本大陪审团查证，兹可断言：近日应合众国总统之请由酋长们交出的四名印第安人，并非谋杀马丁的真凶。酋长们声称，索交令下达之时，真正的罪犯已逃出其部族的疆域与辖制。现押诸犯系自愿顶替逃犯交付；且依印第安人的习俗，他们以为这已足以履行总统的索求。大陪审团不愿断言酋长们存心搪塞，但确实认定：已提出的索求已被规避。」

> 1833 年 6 月 14 日，上述四名印第安人经人身保护令被解送至主持法院的理查德·M·扬法官（Hon. Richard M. Young）面前，当庭开释。

> 公诉书其后指向真凶——沙什-库奥-瓦希（Shash-quo-washi）、穆克-奎-切-夸（Muck-que-che-qua）、穆克-夸-帕尔-阿沙（Muck-qua-pal-ashah）与瓦斯-阿-沃-阿-库奥特（Was-a-wau-a-quot）：他们「目中无敬畏上帝之心，反为魔鬼的怂恿所煽动诱惑」，杀害了威廉·马丁。公诉书由州检察官托马斯·福特（Thomas Ford）拟就，其中陈述威廉·马丁系被枪击于肩胛骨稍下之处。列名证人中有基奥卡克与刺酋（Stabbing Chief）。罪犯始终未曾归案，1835 年十月庭期，本案以*撤销追诉（nolle prosequi）*结案。

### Gen. Scott Arrives at Chicago / 斯科特将军抵达芝加哥

===Original===
Gen. Scott, with a full regiment of regulars, came up the lake and landed at Chicago about the 10th of July﻿—the cholera in the meantime having broken out among his troops, from which several had died. While encamped at that point, it continued its virulence to such an extent, and in a number of cases fatally, that he deemed it best to march out on the high land, and soon after continued his journey, by slow marches, to Rock Island. On reaching Rock river, where Milan is now situated, the cholera had disappeared, and he went into camp with his entire regiment. The clear water of this beautiful stream was a godsend to the many tired men, for the ablution of their bodies and the cleansing of their apparel, tents, etc., and seemed to have a general invigorating effect upon the entire regiment.
Gen. Scott then went over to Rock Island with two companies to garrison Fort Armstrong, and there learned the situation of affairs in the army, and the great reduction made in the ranks of Black Hawk’s band of Indians, so that a final close of the war was daily expected.
A few days after their arrival at Fort Armstrong, symptoms of cholera again appeared among the troops of the company, and the physician in charge tried every known remedy to check it, but failed in every instance, and after running its course, which was usually about twenty-four hours, the patient died. During the first three or four days of its ravages, about one-half of that company had been consigned to their last resting place in the soldiers’ cemetery.
Being on a visit to Rock Island at the time the cholera was raging, the writer, at the request of Col. Wm. Berry, (who had also come down from Galena to pay his respects to Gen. Scott,) accompanied him to the Fort and introduced him to the General. It was a very warm, but beautiful Sabbath, when we were admitted to the General’s quarters, about 10 o’clock in the morning, and after the introduction of our friend and the usual salutations of the day, the General, after expressing his doubts of the propriety of admitting us into the Fort, forcibly and touchingly detailed the ravages that the cholera was making in his ranks. Medicine, in the hands of a skillful physician, seemed to have no effect to stay its progress, and he was just on the eve of trying a different remedy as we came in, and if we would join him in a glass of brandy and water, he would proceed at once to put it into execution. He said he was satisfied that brandy was a good antidote to cholera, and by its use many of his soldiers were still well.

===Chinese===
斯科特将军（Gen. Scott）率一个满编的正规军团溯湖而上，约于 7 月 10 日在芝加哥（Chicago）登岸——其时霍乱已在军中爆发，数名士兵病亡。在该地扎营期间，疫势之烈依然不减，且屡有死亡病例，他遂认为最好是拔营开上高地，不久便以缓慢的行军继续向罗克岛进发。行抵罗克河——即今米兰（Milan）所在地——时，霍乱已然止息，他便率全团扎营。这条美丽的河流那清澈的河水，对许多困乏的士兵真是天赐之惠，可以沐浴身体、浣洗衣物、帐幕等物，似乎使全团上下精神为之一振。

随后斯科特将军带两个连前往罗克岛，驻守阿姆斯特朗堡，在那里了解到军中的局势，以及黑鹰的印第安部众兵力已大为折损，故战争的最后结束指日可待。

他们抵达阿姆斯特朗堡数日之后，这个连的士兵中再度出现霍乱症状；主管军医试遍已知的各种疗法加以遏止，却无一奏效，病人熬完病程——通常约二十四小时——便告不治。疫病肆虐的头三四天里，该连约有一半人已被送入士兵墓园，安置于他们最后的安息之所。

霍乱肆虐之时，笔者恰在罗克岛访问；应威廉·贝里上校（Col. Wm. Berry）之请（他也从加利纳下来拜会斯科特将军），笔者陪他前往堡垒，将他引见给将军。那是天气燠热却十分晴美的一个安息日，上午 10 点左右我们获准进入将军的住所；将我们的朋友引见完毕、互道当日寒暄之后，将军先是表示，让我们入堡是否妥当，他心存疑虑，继而有力而动人地细述霍乱正在他队伍中造成的荼毒。药物到了医术高明的医生手里，似乎也阻不住疫病的进展；我们进来时，他正要试用另一种药方——倘若我们肯陪他喝一杯掺水的白兰地，他便即刻付诸施行。他说他确信白兰地是霍乱的良剂，多亏用了它，他的许多士兵才得以安然无恙。

### The General’s Remedy / 将军的药方

===Original===
The General pulled off his coat, rolled up his sleeves, and directed an orderly to tear off strips of red flannel, fill a bucket with brandy and carry them to the hospital. On arriving at the bedside of a patient he directed him to be stripped, and then with flannel soaked in brandy he rubbed his chest thoroughly, in order to bring on a reaction, in the meantime administering a little brandy with a spoon. In the course of half an hour he returned and reported progress. He said he left his patient free from pain, and directed a small portion of the brandy to be given occasionally.
The well soldiers, seeing that their General was not afraid of cholera, nor too proud to act as nurse to a sick soldier, took courage and insisted on his retiring, so that they could fill his place. Seeing that new life had been infused among the well soldiers, and a gleam of hope seeming to inspire the sick, he gave directions for them to continue, as he had commenced, and then retired.
On returning to his quarters he washed his hands, rolled down his sleeves, put on his uniform, and then invited us to take a little brandy. After listening to his mode of treatment, we casually remarked that it looked feasible, but at the same time reprehensible in the General of the army exposing himself in the performance of a duty that could be done as well by a common soldier. He gave us a look, and kept his eyes upon us as his giant form raised up, and, with a sweep of his sword arm, said in majestic tones: “Sir, it is the duty of a General to take care of his army; should he fall another can take his place; but, without an army his occupation is gone!”
The General’s treatment was continued right along, and the result was that many of those attacked got well.

===Chinese===
将军脱下外衣，卷起袖子，吩咐一名勤务兵撕下红法兰绒布条，装满一桶白兰地，送到医院去。来到病人床边，他吩咐给病人脱去衣衫，然后用浸透白兰地的法兰绒把病人的胸口细细揉擦，以促发身体的反应，同时用汤匙一点点喂他白兰地。约莫半个钟头后他回来了，报告进展：他说他离开时病人已不复疼痛，并嘱咐不时给病人喂少许白兰地。

没患病的士兵们见他们的将军既不惧怕霍乱，又不摆架子、肯屈尊给病兵当看护，人人鼓起勇气，坚请将军退下，由他们来接手。眼见新的生气已注入健康士兵之中，病人们也似有一线希望的微光在激励着他们，将军便嘱咐众人照他开始的方子继续施治，随后退了出来。

回到住所，他洗净双手，放下袖子，穿上军服，然后请我们喝一点白兰地。听罢他的疗法，我们随口说这法子看来行得通，但全军统帅亲冒风险，去做普通士兵同样能做的事，未免有失审慎、应受责备。他看了我们一眼，目光始终不离我们，魁伟的身躯随之站起，持剑的臂膀一挥，以威严的声调说道：「先生，保全大军是统帅的职责；我若倒下，自有他人接替；可若没了大军，统帅的职守便无所依托了！」

将军的疗法一直继续下去，结果是许多染病的人痊愈了。

===Original===
Soon after the close of the war, which terminated with the battle of Bad Axe, on the second day of August, 1832, he came to Galena, and, in conference with Governor John Reynolds, ordered the chiefs, head men and warriors of the Winnebago Nation to meet them at Fort Armstrong, Rock Island, on the 15th day of September, 1832, for the purpose of holding a treaty.
At the time fixed by the Commissioners they were met by the chiefs, head men and warriors of the Winnebago Nation, with whom a treaty was made and concluded, by which the Winnebagoes ceded to the United States all the lands claimed by them lying to the south and east of Wisconsin river and the Fox river of Green Bay. The consideration of this cession on the part of the United States, to be a grant to the Winnebago Nation of a tract on the west side of the Mississippi river known as the neutral ground and annual annuities for twenty-seven years of $10,000 in specie and a further sum, not to exceed $3,000 annually, for the purposes of maintaining a farm and a school for the education of Winnebago children during the same period of twenty-seven years.

===Chinese===
战争以 1832 年 8 月 2 日的巴德阿克斯之战（battle of Bad Axe）告终。战后不久，他来到加利纳，与约翰·雷诺兹州长（Governor John Reynolds）会商，传令温尼贝戈族的酋长、头人和战士于 1832 年 9 月 15 日到罗克岛阿姆斯特朗堡会晤，以缔结条约。

到了专员们指定的日期，温尼贝戈族的酋长、头人和战士如约前来；双方缔结了一项条约，温尼贝戈族据此将其声索的全部位于威斯康星河与格林湾福克斯河（Fox river of Green Bay）以南以东的土地让与合众国。作为此次让与的对价，合众国一方须授予温尼贝戈族密西西比河西岸一片名为「中立地带」（neutral ground）的土地，并连付 27 年、每年 10,000 美元铸币的年金；另在同样这 27 年间，每年拨给一笔不超过 3,000 美元的款项，用于维持一座农庄和一所教育温尼贝戈儿童的学校。

### Treaty with Sacs and Foxes / 与索克族和福克斯族的条约

===Original===
After concluding the treaty with the Winnebagoes, and for the purpose of making a lasting peace with the Sacs and Foxes, these Commissioners held a treaty at the same place, and a week later, on the 21st day of September, with chiefs, head men and warriors of that confederate tribe. The Commissioners demanded, partly as indemnity for expenses incurred in the late war with Black Hawk’s band and to secure future tranquility, a cession of a large portion of their country bordering on the frontiers. In consideration thereof the United States agree to pay to said confederate tribes annually, for thirty years, $20,000 in specie; also, to pay Messrs. Farnham and Davenport, Indian traders at Rock Island, the sum of $40,000, to be receipted for in full of all demands against said Indians. And, further, at the special request of said confederate tribes, the United States agree to grant, by letters patent, to their particular friend, Antoine LeClair, interpreter, one section of land opposite Rock Island and one section at the head of the rapids of the Mississippi river.

===Chinese===
与温尼贝戈族的条约缔结之后，为了同索克族和福克斯族建立长久的和平，诸位专员在同一地点、于一周之后的 9 月 21 日，与这一联盟部族的酋长、头人和战士举行了缔约会议。专员们提出，部分作为近来对黑鹰部众用兵所耗经费的赔偿，部分为确保日后的安宁，要求该部族让出其毗邻边疆的大片国土。作为对价，合众国允诺在此后 30 年间每年付给该联盟部族 20,000 美元铸币；同时付给罗克岛的印第安商人法纳姆与达文波特两位先生（Messrs. Farnham and Davenport）40,000 美元，收款后即出具全数清讫凭据，了结针对上述印第安人的一切债权。此外，应该联盟部族的特别请求，合众国允诺以专利特许状（letters patent），授予他们的一位挚友——译员安托万·勒克莱尔（Antoine LeClair）——罗克岛对岸的一「区」（section，一平方英里的土地测量单位）土地，以及密西西比河急流滩上端的一区土地。

### The City of Davenport, Iowa / 艾奥瓦州达文波特市

===Original===
This beautiful city now covers that “Section of land opposite Rock Island” that was donated by treaty to Antoine LeClair by the Sacs and Foxes, and also three or four more sections. At that time it was wholly uninhabited, the Foxes having removed their village from that point some three years before. As a town site it was regarded by strangers and travelers on steamboats as the most beautiful west of the Mississippi between St. Louis and St. Paul, and now, with its twenty-three thousand inhabitants, elegant residences, magnificent public buildings, fine churches, schoolhouses, extensive manufactories, and large business blocks, it stands unrivalled as a beautiful city. It has ten miles of street railroads, affording easy access to all parts of the city. It has two daily papers, the *Gazette* and *Democrat*, (morning and evening) both ably conducted; and also a German daily and two weeklies. The river is spanned by an elegant bridge that was built at the cost of nearly a million dollars, which is used by the various railroads from East to West, and has a roadway for teams and pedestrians.

===Chinese===
这座美丽的城市如今覆盖的，正是当年由索克族和福克斯族依条约赠予安托万·勒克莱尔的那一「区罗克岛对岸的土地」，另外还占了三四区之地。当年此地全无人居——福克斯族约在三年前已把村子从那里迁走。作为镇址，它被乘汽船而过的生客旅人视为圣路易斯（St. Louis）与圣保罗（St. Paul）之间密西西比河以西最美的一处；如今，它拥有 23,000 名居民、雅致的宅邸、宏丽的公共建筑、精美的教堂与校舍、规模可观的工厂和成片的大型商楼，作为一座美丽之城已无出其右者。市内有 10 英里有轨电车线路，通往全市各处，极为便利。它有两家日报——*《公报》（Gazette）*与*《民主党人报》（Democrat）*（一早一晚），均办得有声有色；另有一家德文日报和两家周报。大河由一座雅致的大桥飞跨，桥以近百万美元建成，东西往返的各条铁路均经行其上，桥面另辟有车马与行人的通道。

### The City of Rock Island / 罗克岛市

===Original===
is located on the bank of the river in Illinois, immediately opposite to Davenport, and is a large and flourishing city, with a population of about twelve thousand inhabitants. It has fine public buildings, elegant churches and residences, substantial business blocks, extensive manufactories and elegant water works. The city is lighted by electric lights, from high towers, that cast their refulgent rays over the entire city, which makes it the finest lighted city in the west. There are two daily papers, (morning and evening) *The Union* and *The Argus*, both enjoying the privilege of Press dispatches, and both issue weeklies. *The Rock Islander* is also published weekly, and all have the appearance of great prosperity. The professions are represented by men of fine ability, including some of wide reputation. The banking business is done principally by two National Banks, that have a deservedly high reputation, and are doing a large business. There are two first-class hotels﻿—the Harper House and Rock Island House﻿—and several of less pretentions. The city has large coal fields, in close proximity, with railroads running daily to and from the banks, by which the three cities are supplied.

===Chinese===
位于伊利诺伊州境内的河岸上，与达文波特隔水相望，是一座繁荣的大城，居民约 12,000 人。城中有精美的公共建筑、雅致的教堂与宅邸、坚固的商楼、规模可观的工厂和精美的自来水设施。全城由高塔上的电灯照明，璀璨的光芒普照全城，使它成为西部灯火最辉煌的城市。这里有两家日报（一早一晚）——*《联盟报》（The Union）*与*《阿耳戈斯报》（The Argus）*，都享有通讯社电讯的特权，也都发行周报；*《罗克岛人报》（The Rock Islander）*亦每周刊行，各家报馆都显得一派兴旺。各专业行当皆有才俊执业，其中不乏名重一方之士。银行业务主要由两家国民银行经营，其声誉之高当之无愧，业务量可观。城里有两家一流旅馆——哈珀旅馆（Harper House）与罗克岛旅馆（Rock Island House）——另有几家规模稍逊者。城市近旁有大片煤田，铁路每日往来于矿场之间，三座城市的用煤均赖此供给。

### The City of Moline / 莫林市

===Original===
is located two miles up the river from Rock Island, but connected with it by street railways. It has a population of over 8,000 inhabitants, and is extensively known from its many manufacturing establishments, which are supplied with water power from a dam across the river from the Island.

===Chinese===
位于罗克岛溯河而上两英里处，但有有轨电车线路与罗克岛相连。居民 8,000 有余，以众多的制造工场闻名遐迩——这些工场的动力，来自一道从岛边横锁河面的水坝所提供的水力。

### Fifty Years Ago / 五十年前

===Original===
when the writer first visited this most beautiful Island in the Mississippi river, then and now known as Rock Island, the ground on which the triplet cities of Davenport, Rock Island, and Moline now stands, was covered with prairie grass, and apparently a sterile waste as regards to the two former, whilst the latter was principally covered with timber. Now how changed! Then the site of Davenport was claimed to be the most beautiful on the west bank of the Mississippi, between St. Paul and St. Louis by Black Hawk and his confreres, who had traveled up and down the river in canoes, whilst his judgment was confirmed by thousands of passengers who viewed it from steamboats in after years.

===Chinese===
笔者初访密西西比河上这座最美的岛——当时与如今都叫作罗克岛——的时候，达文波特、罗克岛与莫林这三座三联城今日所踞的土地，还覆盖着草原的荒草：前两城所在，望去似是一片贫瘠的荒野；后一城所在，则主要为林木覆盖。如今变化何其大也！当年，黑鹰和他的同侪们——他们曾乘独木舟在河上往来上下游历——就断言达文波特所在的地址，是圣保罗与圣路易斯之间密西西比河西岸最美的一处；他这一评判，后来也为年复一年从汽船上眺望此地的千万旅客所证实。

### The Triple Cities / 三联城

===Original===
are widely known as the leading manufacturing cities of the great west, with railroads stretching out from ocean to ocean, and although the Mississippi makes a dividing line, they are united by a magnificent bridge, which makes their intercourse easier than over paved streets.
Rock Island, at that time, was excluded from settlement by the orders of Government, as it had been reserved, on the recommendation of Hon. Lewis Cass, whilst he was in the Senate and Cabinet, as a site for a United States Arsenal and Armory. Fort Armstrong was situated on the lower end of the Island, and was then in command of Col. William Davenport. The Sac and Fox agency (Maj. Davenport, agent,) stood on the bank of the river about half a mile above the Fort; next came the residence and office of Antoine Le Clair, United States Interpreter for the Sam and Foxes, and a little higher up, the residence, storehouse and out buildings of Col. George Davenport, who had by an act of Congress, preempted a claim of two hundred acres of land running across the Island from bank to bank of the river. The Island is about two miles long, and being at the foot of the rapids has the best water power on the river, capable of running a much greater amount of machinery than is at present in operation. The entire Island is now owned and occupied by the Government, (the heirs of Col. Davenport having sold and deeded their interest), and is now used as an

===Chinese===
作为大西部首屈一指的工业城市广为人知，铁路自此伸展开去，通达两洋；密西西比河虽把它们隔作两岸，一座宏丽的大桥又将它们连为一体，彼此往来之便，更胜于行走于铺石的街道。

罗克岛当年奉政府之命禁止定居——它已依刘易斯·卡斯先生（Hon. Lewis Cass）——他在参议院和内阁任职期间——的举荐，被保留作合众国军械库与兵工厂的所在。阿姆斯特朗堡位于岛的下游一端，当时由威廉·达文波特上校（Col. William Davenport）统辖。索克-福克斯族事务代办处（代办为达文波特少校）设在河岸上、堡垒上游约半英里处；再往上是合众国驻索克-福克斯族译员安托万·勒克莱尔的宅邸与办公处；更往上一点，是乔治·达文波特上校（Col. George Davenport）的住宅、货栈和附属屋舍——他已依国会的一项法令，取得了一片横贯全岛、从河的一岸到另一岸共 200 英亩土地的优先购买权。此岛约两英里长，地处急流滩下端，拥有全河最好的水力，足以带动比现下运转中的机器多得多的机器。全岛如今为政府所有并占用（达文波特上校的继承人已将其权益出售并立契让渡），现用作——

### Armory and Arsenal, / 兵工厂与军械库，

===Original===
which are destined to be in the near future, the most extensive works of the kind probably in the world. Indeed, army officers who have traveled extensively in the Old World, say they have never seen anything to compare with it, in elegant grounds, water power and buildings, and with such facilities for moving anything to and from the Arsenal. These works were commenced under the supervision of Gen. Rodman, the inventor of the Rodman gun, and since the death of the General, D. W. Flagler, Lieut. Col. of Ordinance, has been in command, and a more efficient and better qualified officer for the place could not have been found in the army.
There are already completed ten massive stone buildings, which are used for work shops, storage, etc., officers’ quarters, both durable and comfortable, and many other buildings. The former residence of Col. George Davenport, (the House in which he was killed for money many years ago) built in 1831, of solid hewed timber, and afterwards weather-boarded, still stands unoccupied.
The Island is mostly covered with trees of different varieties, which are kept neatly trimmed, and is laid out like a park, with wide avenues extending its whole length, which makes the most elegant drives and shady walks for the thousands of visitors who flock to the Island to feast their eyes upon its magnificence.

===Chinese===
在不久的将来，注定会成为世界上同类工程中规模最巨者。的确，曾遍游旧大陆的陆军军官们说，就场地之雅致、水力之盛与建筑之美而言，连同往来军械库转运任何物资的便利而言，他们从未见过堪与此地相比者。这些工程在罗德曼将军（Gen. Rodman）——罗德曼炮的发明者——的督理下动工；将军身后，由军械中校 D·W·弗拉格勒（D. W. Flagler）主持其事，全军之中再也找不出比他更称职、更干练的军官来执掌此地了。

目前已建成 10 座巨大的石砌建筑，用作工场、库房等；又有军官宿舍，坚固而舒适；此外还有许多其他建筑。乔治·达文波特上校的旧居（多年前他便是为钱财在那座房子里遇害的）建于 1831 年，以整根斫削的实木构筑，外墙后来加钉了雨淋板，至今仍空置着。

岛上大部分地面覆盖着各式树木，修剪得整整齐齐，全岛布置得如同一座园林，宽阔的林荫大道贯通首尾，为成千上万涌到岛上一饱眼福、观赏其壮丽的游人，提供了最优美的车道与浓荫步径。

### The City of Keokuk, Iowa / 艾奥瓦州基奥卡克市

===Original===
is located at the foot of the Lower Rapids, 139 miles from Rock Island, and bears the name of the distinguished chief of the Sacs and Foxes. At our first visit there, in 1832, there was a long row of one-story buildings fronting on the river, that were used by Col. Farnham, agent of the American Fur Company, as a store and warehouse﻿—this being the principal depot for trade with the Sacs and Foxes, who were then the sole proprietors of the country and its principal inhabitants, with the exception of a few individuals who had got permission to put up shanties for occupation during the low-water season, while they were engaged in lighting steamers passing up and down the river, but unable to cross the rapids while loaded.
At that day the old chief, Keokuk, boasted of having the handsomest site for a big village that could be found on the river, and since that day it has grown to be a large and elegant city, with wide streets, fine public buildings, nice churches, schoolhouses, elegant residences, extensive business houses, wholesale and retail stores, manufactories, and a flourishing Medical University with elegant buildings, which has been in successful operation for more than twenty years. The United States District Court for Southern Iowa is also located here. The city is well provided with good hotels. The Patterson House, an immense building, five stories high, being chief, which has always ranked as first-class﻿—with a number of hotels of smaller dimensions, but well kept﻿—affording ample accommodation for the thousands of travelers that frequently congregate at this place. The various professions are represented by men of fine ability﻿—some of them of wide reputation. They have two daily papers, *The Gale City*, and *The Constitution*, which are ably conducted.
A fine canal, running the entire length of the Rapids, from Montrose to Keokuk, has been built by the United States, through which steamboats can now pass at any stage of water﻿—but designed more particularly for low water﻿—so that there is no longer any detention to lighten steamboats over the Rapids.

===Chinese===
位于下急流滩的末端，距罗克岛 139 英里，以索克族与福克斯族那位杰出酋长之名为名。1832 年我们初访此地时，河边只有一长排单层的房子，是美国皮毛公司代理人法纳姆上校（Col. Farnham）用作商行和货栈的——那是与索克族和福克斯族贸易的主要集散地；当时两族还是这片土地唯一的主人，也是当地主要的居民，只有少数几个人获准搭起棚屋，在枯水季节栖身——他们受雇为往来河上的汽船引航领水，因为满载的汽船无法越过急流滩。

当年，老酋长基奥卡克曾夸口说，他拥有全河所能寻见的最漂亮的大村址；自那时以来，这里已长成一座宏大而雅致的城市，街道宽阔，公共建筑精美，教堂、校舍漂亮，宅邸考究，大型商号、批零店铺与工厂林立，还有一所建筑雅致、蒸蒸日上的医科大学，已成功经营 20 多年。合众国南艾奥瓦联邦地区法院也设在此地。城中好旅馆充足：以帕特森旅馆（Patterson House）为首——那是一座五层高的巨大建筑，历来位列一流——另有若干规模较小但打理得当的旅馆，足以为时常云集此地的成千上万旅客提供充裕的住宿。各专业行当皆有才俊执业，其中不乏名重一方之士。这里有两家日报——*《风城报》（The Gale City）*与*《宪法报》（The Constitution）*，都办得有声有色。

合众国已建成一条优良的运河，沿急流滩全程、自蒙特罗斯（Montrose）直通基奥卡克；汽船如今无论水位高低皆可经此通行——但此渠尤专为枯水而设——自此再不必滞留滩前、卸轻船货以过急流滩了。

### The City of Muscatine, Iowa / 艾奥瓦州马斯卡廷市

===Original===
Muscatine was first settled as a wood yard by Col. John Vanater, in July, 1834, and was laid out as a town by him in 1836, and called Bloomington. The county was organized in 1837, under the name of Muscatine, and Bloomington made the county seat. The name of the town was changed to correspond with that of the county in 1851.
